<li><p>Identifying irregular sign-in activity</p></li>
<li><p>Configuring multi-factor authentication</p></li>
<li><p>Extending existing on-premises Active Directory implementations to Microsoft Entra ID</p></li>
<li><p>Configuring Application Proxy for cloud and local applications</p></li>
<li><p>Configuring Conditional Access for users and devices</p></li>
</ul>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image43.webp" style="width:5.59717in;height:2.34846in" alt="Diagram that shows the Microsoft Entra Connect Stack." /></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra constitutes a separate Azure service.</p></li>
<li><p>Its <strong>most elementary form</strong>, which any new Azure subscription includes automatically, doesn't incur any extra cost and is referred to as the Free tier.</p></li>
<li><p>If you subscribe to any Microsoft Online business services (for example, Microsoft 365 or Microsoft Intune), you automatically get Microsoft Entra ID with access to all the Free features.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Note:</strong> By default, when you create a new Azure subscription by using a Microsoft account, the subscription automatically includes a new Microsoft Entra tenant named Default Directory.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Some of the more advanced identity management features require paid versions of Microsoft Entra ID, offered in the form of Basic and Premium tiers.</p></li>
<li><p>Some of these features are also automatically included in Microsoft Entra instances generated as part of Microsoft 365 subscriptions.</p></li>
<li><p>Differences between Microsoft Entra versions are discussed later in this module.</p></li>
<li><p>Implementing Microsoft Entra ID isn't the same as deploying virtual machines in Azure, adding AD DS, and then deploying some domain controllers for a new forest and domain.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID is a different service, much more focused on providing identity management services to web-based apps, unlike AD DS, which is more focused on on-premises apps.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Microsoft Entra tenants</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Unlike AD DS, Microsoft Entra ID is <strong>multi-tenant by design</strong> and is implemented specifically to ensure isolation between its individual directory instances.</p></li>
<li><p>It’s the <strong>world’s largest multi-tenant directory</strong>, hosting over a million directory services instances, with billions of authentication requests per week.</p></li>
<li><p>The term <strong>tenant</strong> in this context typically represents a <strong>company or organization</strong> that signed up for a subscription to a Microsoft cloud-based service such as Microsoft 365, Intune, or Azure, each of which uses Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
<li><p>However, from a technical standpoint, the term tenant represents an individual Microsoft Entra instance.</p></li>
<li><p>Within an Azure subscription, you can create multiple Microsoft Entra tenants.</p></li>
<li><p>Having multiple Microsoft Entra tenants might be convenient if you want to test Microsoft Entra functionality in one tenant without affecting the others.</p></li>
<li><p>At any given time, an Azure subscription must be associated with one, and only one, Microsoft Entra tenant.</p></li>
<li><p>This association allows you to grant permissions to resources in the Azure subscription (via RBAC) to users, groups, and applications that exist in that particular Microsoft Entra tenant.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Note:</strong> You can associate the same Microsoft Entra tenant with multiple Azure subscriptions. This allows you to use the same users, groups, and applications to manage resources across multiple Azure subscriptions.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Each Microsoft Entra tenant is assigned the default Domain Name System (DNS) domain name, consisting of a <strong>unique prefix</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>The prefix, derived from the name of the Microsoft account you use to create an Azure subscription or provided explicitly when creating a Microsoft Entra tenant, is followed by the <strong>onmicrosoft.com</strong> suffix.</p></li>
<li><p>Adding at least one custom domain name to the same Microsoft Entra tenant is possible and common.</p></li>
<li><p>This name utilizes the DNS domain namespace that the corresponding company or organization owns.</p></li>
<li><p>The Microsoft Entra tenant serves as the security boundary and a container for Microsoft Entra objects such as users, groups, and applications.</p></li>
<li><p>A single Microsoft Entra tenant can support multiple Azure subscriptions.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Microsoft Entra schema</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>The Microsoft Entra schema contains fewer object types than that of AD DS.</p></li>
<li><p>Most notably, it doesn't include a definition of the computer class, although it does include the device class.</p></li>
<li><p>The process of joining devices to Microsoft Entra differs considerably from the process of joining computers to AD DS.</p></li>
<li><p>The Microsoft Entra schema is also easily extensible, and its extensions are fully reversible.</p></li>
<li><p>The lack of support for the traditional computer domain membership means that you can't use Microsoft Entra ID to manage computers or user settings by using traditional management techniques, such as Group Policy Objects (GPOs).</p></li>
<li><p>Instead, Microsoft Entra ID and its services define a concept of modern management.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID’s primary strength lies in providing directory services; storing and publishing user, device, and application data; and handling the authentication and authorization of the users, devices, and applications.</p></li>
<li><p>The effectiveness and efficiency of these features are apparent based on existing deployments of cloud services such as Microsoft 365, which rely on Microsoft Entra ID as their identity provider and support millions of users.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID <strong>doesn't include the organizational unit (OU)</strong> class, which means that you can't arrange its objects into a hierarchy of custom containers, which is frequently used in on-premises AD DS deployments.</p></li>
<li><p>However, this isn't a significant shortcoming, because OUs in AD DS are used primarily for Group Policy scoping and delegation.</p></li>
<li><p>You can accomplish equivalent arrangements by organizing objects based on their group membership.</p></li>
<li><p>Objects of the Application and servicePrincipal classes represent applications in Microsoft Entra ID. An object in the Application class contains an application definition and an object in the servicePrincipal class constitutes its instance in the current Microsoft Entra tenant.</p></li>
<li><p>Separating these two sets of characteristics allows you to define an application in one tenant and use it across multiple tenants by creating a service principal object for this application in each tenant.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID creates the service principal object when you register the corresponding application in that Microsoft Entra tenant.</p></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Compare Microsoft Entra ID and Active Directory Domain Services

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>You could view Microsoft Entra ID simply as the cloud-based counterpart of AD DS; however, while Microsoft Entra ID and AD DS share some common characteristics, there are several significant differences between them.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Characteristics of AD DS</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>AD DS is the traditional deployment of <strong>Windows Server-based Active Directory</strong> on a physical or virtual server.</p></li>
<li><p>Although AD DS is commonly considered to be primarily a directory service, it’s only one component of the Windows Active Directory suite of technologies, which also includes</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Active Directory Certificate Services (AD CS),</p></li>
<li><p>Active Directory Lightweight Directory Services (AD LDS),</p></li>
<li><p>Active Directory Federation Services (AD FS), and</p></li>
<li><p>Active Directory Rights Management Services (AD RMS).</p></li>
</ul></li>
</ul>
<p>When comparing AD DS with Microsoft Entra ID, it’s important to note the following characteristics of AD DS:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>AD DS is a true directory service, with a hierarchical X.500-based structure.</p></li>
<li><p>AD DS uses Domain Name System (DNS) for locating resources such as domain controllers.</p></li>
<li><p>You can query and manage AD DS by using Lightweight Directory Access Protocol (LDAP) calls.</p></li>
<li><p>AD DS primarily uses the Kerberos protocol for authentication.</p></li>
<li><p>AD DS uses OUs and GPOs for management.</p></li>
<li><p>AD DS includes computer objects, representing computers that join an Active Directory domain.</p></li>
<li><p>AD DS uses trusts between domains for delegated management.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>You can deploy AD DS on an Azure virtual machine to enable scalability and availability for an on-premises AD DS. However, deploying AD DS on an Azure virtual machine doesn't make any use of Microsoft Entra ID.</p>
<p><strong> Note:</strong> Deploying AD DS on an Azure virtual machine requires one or more extra Azure data disks because you shouldn't use drive C for AD DS storage. These disks are needed to store the AD DS database, logs, and the sysvol folder. The Host Cache Preference setting for these disks must be set to None.</p>
<p><strong>Characteristics of Microsoft Entra ID</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Although Microsoft Entra ID has many similarities to AD DS, there are also <strong>many differences</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>It’s important to realize that using Microsoft Entra isn’t the same as deploying an Active Directory domain controller on an Azure virtual machine and adding it to your on-premises domain.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>When comparing Microsoft Entra ID with AD DS, it’s important to note the following characteristics of Microsoft Entra ID:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID is primarily an identity solution, and it’s designed for internet-based applications by using HTTP (port 80) and HTTPS (port 443) communications.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID is a multi-tenant directory service.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra users and groups are created in a <strong>flat structure</strong>, and there are no OUs or GPOs.</p></li>
<li><p>You <strong>can't query Microsoft Entra ID by using LDAP</strong>; instead, Microsoft Entra ID uses the REST API over HTTP and HTTPS.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID doesn't use Kerberos authentication;</p>
<ul>
<li><p>instead, it uses HTTP and HTTPS protocols such as SAML, WS-Federation, and OpenID Connect for authentication, and uses OAuth for authorization.</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID includes federation services, and many third-party services such as Facebook are federated with and trust Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Examine Microsoft Entra ID as a directory service for cloud apps

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>When you deploy cloud services such as Microsoft 365 or Intune, you also need to have directory services in the cloud to provide authentication and authorization for these services.</p></li>
<li><p>Because of this, each cloud service that needs authentication will create its own Microsoft Entra tenant.</p></li>
<li><p>When a single organization uses more than one cloud service, it’s much more convenient for these cloud services to use a single cloud directory instead of having separate directories for each service.</p></li>
<li><p>It’s now possible to have one identity service that covers all Microsoft cloud-based services, such as Microsoft 365, Azure, Microsoft Dynamics 365, and Intune.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID provides developers with centralized authentication and authorization for applications in Azure by using other identity providers or on-premises AD DS.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID can provide users with an SSO experience when using applications such as Facebook, Google services, Yahoo, or Microsoft cloud services.</p></li>
<li><p>The process of implementing Microsoft Entra ID support for custom applications is rather complex and beyond the scope of this course.</p></li>
<li><p>However, the Azure portal and Microsoft Visual Studio 2013 and later make the process of configuring such support more straightforward.</p></li>
<li><p>In particular, you can enable Microsoft Entra authentication for the Web Apps feature of Azure App Service directly from the Authentication/Authorization blade in the Azure portal. By designating the Microsoft Entra tenant, you can ensure that only users with accounts in that directory can access the website. It’s possible to apply different authentication settings to individual deployment slots.</p></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Compare Microsoft Entra ID P1 and P2 plans

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>The Microsoft Entra ID P1 or P2 tier provides extra functionality as compared to the Free and Office 365 editions.</p></li>
<li><p>However, premium versions require additional cost per user provisioning.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID P1 or P2 comes in two versions P1 and P2.</p></li>
<li><p>You can procure it as an extra license or as a part of the Microsoft Enterprise Mobility + Security, which also includes the license for Azure Information Protection and Intune.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Microsoft provides a free trial period that can be used to experience the full functionality of the Microsoft Entra ID P2 edition. The following features are available with the Microsoft Entra ID P1 edition:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Self-service group management</strong>. It simplifies the administration of groups where users are given the rights to create and manage the groups. End users can create requests to join other groups, and group owners can approve requests and maintain their groups’ memberships.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Advanced security reports and alerts</strong>. You can monitor and protect access to your cloud applications by viewing detailed logs that show advanced anomalies and inconsistent access pattern reports. Advanced reports are machine learning based and can help you gain new insights to improve access security and respond to potential threats.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Multi-factor authentication</strong>. Full multi-factor authentication (MFA) works with on-premises applications (using virtual private network [VPN], RADIUS, and others), Azure, Microsoft 365, Dynamics 365, and third-party Microsoft Entra gallery applications. It doesn't work with non-browser off-the-shelf apps, such as Microsoft Outlook. Full multi-factor authentication is covered in more detail in the following units in this lesson.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Microsoft Identity Manager (MIM) licensing</strong>. MIM integrates with Microsoft Entra ID P1 or P2 to provide hybrid identity solutions. MIM can bridge multiple on-premises authentication stores such as AD DS, LDAP, Oracle, and other applications with Microsoft Entra ID. This provides consistent experiences to on-premises line-of-business (LOB) applications and SaaS solutions.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Enterprise SLA of 99.9%</strong>. You're guaranteed at least 99.9% availability of the Microsoft Entra ID P1 or P2 service. The same SLA applies to Microsoft Entra Basic.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Password reset with writeback</strong>. Self-service password reset follows the Active Directory on-premises password policy.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Cloud App Discovery feature of Microsoft Entra ID</strong>. This feature discovers the most frequently used cloud-based applications.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Conditional Access based on device, group, or location</strong>. This lets you configure conditional access for critical resources, based on several criteria.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Microsoft Entra Connect Health</strong>. You can use this tool to gain operational insight into Microsoft Entra ID. It works with alerts, performance counters, usage patterns, and configuration settings, and presents the collected information in the Microsoft Entra Connect Health portal.</p></li>
<li></li>
</ul>
<p>In addition to these features, the Microsoft Entra ID P2 license provides extra functionalities:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Microsoft Entra ID Protection</strong>. This feature provides enhanced functionalities for monitoring and protecting user accounts. You can define user risk policies and sign-in policies. In addition, you can review users’ behavior and flag users for risk.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Microsoft Entra Privileged Identity Management</strong>. This functionality lets you configure additional security levels for privileged users such as administrators. With Privileged Identity Management, you define permanent and temporary administrators. You also define a policy workflow that activates whenever someone wants to use administrative privileges to perform some task.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong> Note:</strong> Plans change frequently. Check Microsoft's website for the current plans and capabilities.</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Examine Microsoft Entra Domain Services

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>In most organizations today, line-of-business (LOB) applications are deployed on computers and devices that are domain members.</p></li>
<li><p>These organizations use AD DS–based credentials for authentication, and Group Policy manages them.</p></li>
<li><p>When you consider moving these apps to run in Azure, one key issue is how to provide authentication services to these apps.</p></li>
<li><p>To satisfy this need, you can choose to implement a site-to-site virtual private network (VPN) between your local infrastructure and the Azure IaaS, or you can deploy replica domain controllers from your local AD DS as virtual machines (VMs) in Azure.</p></li>
<li><p>These approaches can entail additional costs and administrative effort.</p></li>
<li><p>Additionally, the difference between these two approaches is that with the first option, authentication traffic will cross the VPN, while in the second option, replication traffic will cross the VPN and authentication traffic stays in the cloud.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Microsoft provides Microsoft Entra Domain Services as an alternative to these approaches. This service, which runs as part of the Microsoft Entra ID P1 or P2 tier, provides domain services such as Group Policy management, domain joining, and Kerberos authentication to your Microsoft Entra tenant. These services are fully compatible with locally deployed AD DS, so you can use them without deploying and managing additional domain controllers in the cloud.</p>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image44.webp" style="width:5.72481in;height:2.96842in" alt="Diagram that shows the Microsoft Entra Domain Services Overview." /></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Because Microsoft Entra ID can integrate with your local AD DS, when you implement Microsoft Entra Connect, users can utilize organizational credentials in both on-premises AD DS and in Microsoft Entra Domain Services.</p></li>
<li><p>Even if you don’t have AD DS deployed locally, you can choose to use Microsoft Entra Domain Services as a cloud-only service.</p></li>
<li><p>This enables you to have similar functionality of locally deployed AD DS without having to deploy a single domain controller on-premises or in the cloud.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, an organization can choose to create a Microsoft Entra tenant and enable Microsoft Entra Domain Services, and then deploy a virtual network between its on-premises resources and the Microsoft Entra tenant.</p></li>
<li><p>You can enable Microsoft Entra Domain Services for this virtual network so that all on-premises users and services can use domain services from Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Microsoft Entra Domain Services provides several benefits for organizations, such as:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Administrators don't need to manage, update, and monitor domain controllers.</p></li>
<li><p>Administrators don't need to deploy and manage Active Directory replication.</p></li>
<li><p>There’s no need to have Domain Admins or Enterprise Admins groups for domains that Microsoft Entra ID manages.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>If you choose to implement Microsoft Entra Domain Services, you need to be aware of the service's current limitations. These include:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Only the base computer Active Directory object is supported.</p></li>
<li><p>It’s not possible to extend the schema for the Microsoft Entra Domain Services domain.</p></li>
<li><p>The organizational unit (OU) structure is flat and nested OUs aren't currently supported.</p></li>
<li><p>There’s a built-in Group Policy Object (GPO), and it exists for computer and user accounts.</p></li>
<li><p>It’s not possible to target OUs with built-in GPOs. Additionally, you can't use Windows Management Instrumentation filters or security-group filtering.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>By using Microsoft Entra Domain Services, you can freely migrate applications that use LDAP, NTLM, or the Kerberos protocols from your on-premises infrastructure to the cloud. You can also use applications such as Microsoft SQL Server or Microsoft SharePoint Server on VMs or deploy them in the Azure IaaS, without needing domain controllers in the cloud or a VPN to local infrastructure.</p>
<p>You can enable Microsoft Entra Domain Services by using the Azure portal. This service charges per hour based on the size of your directory.</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Create, configure, and manage identities

|     |
|:----|
|     |

### Introduction 

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>Transitioning workloads to the cloud involves more than just moving servers, websites, and data.</p></li>
<li><p>Companies need to think about how to secure those resources, by defining authorized users.</p></li>
<li><p>Next, companies need to ensure that users only have access to data that they need, that user authorization is limited only create services available to them, and that users only perform operations authorized for them to perform.</p></li>
<li><p>Access to cloud-based workloads is controlled centrally in two ways.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>First</strong> by providing a definitive identity for each user that they use for every service.</p></li>
<li><p>Second, by ensuring employees and vendors have enough access to do their jobs.</p></li>
<li><p>Azure helps to make these sorts of challenges easier to solve with Microsoft Entra ID, the Microsoft cloud-based identity and access management service.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID provides end-to-end identity management; including single sign-on and multifactor authentication to help protect your users and your data. In this module, you learn the basics of creating, configuring, and managing users and groups of users. You'll also learn how to manage licenses.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Learning objectives</strong></p>
<p>In this module, you'll:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Create, configure, and manage users</p></li>
<li><p>Create, configure, and manage groups</p></li>
<li><p>Manage licenses</p></li>
<li><p>Explore custom security attributes and automatic provisioning</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Prerequisites</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Basic understand of identity management</p></li>
<li><p>Some experience with Active Directory a plus</p></li>
<li><p>Experience with Zero Trust helpful</p></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Create, configure, and manage users

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>Every user who needs access to Azure resources need an Azure user account.</p></li>
<li><p>A user account contains all the information needed to authenticate the user during the sign-on process.</p></li>
<li><p>Once authenticated Microsoft Entra ID builds an access token to authorize the user and determine what resources they can access and what they can do with those resources.</p></li>
<li><p>You use the <strong>Microsoft Entra ID</strong> dashboard in the Azure portal to work with user objects.</p></li>
<li><p>Keep in mind that you can only work with a single directory at a time.</p></li>
<li><p>You can use the <strong>Directory + Subscription</strong> panel to switch directories.</p></li>
<li><p>The dashboard also has a <strong>Switch directory</strong> button in the toolbar which makes it easy to switch to another available directory.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>View users</strong></p>
<p>To view the Microsoft Entra users, select the <strong>Users</strong> entry under <strong>Identity</strong> - then open the <strong>All Users</strong> view. Take a minute to access the portal and view your users. Notice the <strong>User Type</strong> column to see members and guests, as the following figure depicts.</p>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image45.webp" style="width:7.6875in;height:3.26875in" alt="Screenshot of the All Users page of Microsoft Entra ID. See users and information about them." /></p>
<p>Typically, Microsoft Entra ID defines users in three ways:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Cloud identities</strong> - These users exist only in Microsoft Entra ID.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Examples are administrator accounts and users that you manage yourself.</p></li>
<li><p>Their source is <strong>Microsoft Entra ID</strong> or <strong>External Microsoft Entra directory</strong> if the user is defined in another Microsoft Entra instance but needs access to subscription resources controlled by this directory.</p></li>
<li><p>When these accounts are removed from the primary directory, they're deleted.</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><strong>Directory-synchronized identities</strong> </p>
<ul>
<li><p>These users exist in an on-premises Active Directory.</p></li>
<li><p>A synchronization activity that occurs via <strong>Microsoft Entra Connect</strong> brings these users in to Azure.</p></li>
<li><p>Their source is <strong>Windows Server AD</strong>.</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><strong>Guest users</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>These users exist outside Azure.</p></li>
<li><p>Examples are accounts from other cloud providers and Microsoft accounts such as an Xbox LIVE account.</p></li>
<li><p>Their source is <strong>Invited user</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>This type of account is useful when external vendors or contractors need access to your Azure resources.</p></li>
<li><p>Once their help is no longer necessary, you can remove the account and all of their access.</p></li>
</ul></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<li><p>In the <strong>All groups</strong> list, select <strong>Marketing</strong>.</p></li>

<li><p>Select <strong>Billing</strong> from the menu on the left.</p></li>
<li><p>Select <strong>Licenses</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>From the list of licenses you have available, select one.</p></li>
<li><p>Next you'll select <strong>Groups</strong> from the list near the top of the screen.</p></li>
<li><p>On the Groups page, select + <strong>Assign license</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Search for and select the <strong>Marketing</strong> group we created earlier.</p></li>
<li><p>Select the <strong>Assign</strong> button at the bottom of the dialog.</p></li>
<li><p>You should get a message that licenses were successfully assigned.</p></li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Restore or remove a recently deleted user with Microsoft Entra ID</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>After you delete a user, the account remains in a suspended state for 30 days.</p></li>
<li><p>During that 30-day window, the user account can be restored, along with all its properties.</p></li>
<li><p>After that 30-day window passes, the permanent deletion process is automatically started.</p></li>
<li><p>You can view your restorable users, restore a deleted user, or permanently delete a user using Microsoft Entra ID user interface.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Important:</strong> Neither you nor Microsoft customer support can restore a permanently deleted user.</p>
<p><strong>Required permissions</strong></p>
<p>You must have one of the following roles to restore or permanently delete users.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Global administrator</p></li>
<li><p>Partner Tier-1 Support</p></li>
<li><p>Partner Tier-2 Support</p></li>
<li><p>User administrator</p></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>

</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Exercise - restore or remove deleted users

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><p><strong>Remove a user from Microsoft Entra ID</strong></p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>Browse to the <a href="https://entra.microsoft.com/">Microsoft Entra admin center</a>.</p></li>
<li><p>In the left navigation, under <strong>Identity</strong>, select <strong>Users</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>In the <strong>Users</strong> list, select the check box for a user that will be deleted. For example, select <strong>Chris Green</strong>.</p></li>
</ol>
<p><strong> Tip</strong></p>
<p>Selecting users from the list allows you to manage multiple users at the same time. If you select the user, to open that user’s page, you will only be managing that individual user.</p>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image47.webp" style="width:7.6875in;height:4.55556in" alt="Screenshot of Microsoft Entra ID all users&#39; list with one user check box selected." /></p>
<ol start="4" type="1">
<li><p>With the user account selected, on the menu, select <strong>Delete user</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Review the dialog box and then select <strong>OK</strong>.</p></li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Restore a deleted user</strong></p>
<p>You can see all the users that were deleted less than 30 days ago. These users can be restored.</p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>In the Users page, in the left navigation, select <strong>Deleted users</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Review the list of deleted users and select the user you deleted.</p></li>
</ol>
<p><strong> Important</strong></p>
<p>By default, deleted user accounts are permanently removed from Microsoft Entra ID automatically after 30 days.</p>
<ol start="3" type="1">
<li><p>On the menu, select <strong>Restore user</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Review the dialog box and then select <strong>OK</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>In the left navigation, select <strong>All users</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Verify the user has been restored.</p></li>
</ol></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Create, configure, and manage groups

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>A Microsoft Entra group helps organize users, which makes it easier to manage permissions.</p></li>
<li><p>Using groups lets the resource owner (or Microsoft Entra directory owner), assign a set of access permissions to all the members of the group, instead of having to provide the rights one-by-one.</p></li>
<li><p>Groups allow us to define a security boundary and then add and remove specific users to grant or deny access with a minimum amount of effort.</p></li>
<li><p>Even better, Microsoft Entra ID supports the ability to define membership based on rules - such as what department a user works in, or the job title they have.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Microsoft Entra ID allows you to define two different types of groups.</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Security groups</strong>:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>the most <strong>common</strong> type of groups and are used to <strong>manage member and computer access</strong> to shared resources for a group of users.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, you can create a security group for a specific security policy.</p></li>
<li><p>By doing it this way, you can give a set of permissions to all the members at once, instead of having to add permissions to each member individually.</p></li>
<li><p>This option requires a Microsoft Entra administrator.</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><strong>Microsoft 365 groups</strong> </p>
<ul>
<li><p>provide collaboration opportunities by giving members access to a <strong>shared mailbox, calendar, files, SharePoint site, and more.</strong></p></li>
<li><p>This option also lets you give people outside of your organization access to the group.</p></li>
<li><p>This option is available to users as well as admins.</p></li>
</ul></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>View available groups</strong></p>
<p>You can view all groups through the <strong>Groups</strong> item under <strong>Identity</strong> in the Microsoft Entra admin center. A new Microsoft Entra ID deployment won't have any groups defined.</p>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image48.webp" style="width:7.6875in;height:3.12847in" alt="Screenshot of the Microsoft Entra ID view all groups page. Shows a list of several groups." /></p>
<p>The second characteristic of a group that you need to be aware of is the <strong>Membership Type</strong>. This specifies how individuals members are added to the group. The two types are:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Assigned</strong> - members are added and maintained manually.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Dynamic</strong> - members are added based on rules, creating a Dynamic Group. These groups are still either a security group or Microsoft 365 group, just their members are controlled by rule.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Dynamic groups</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>The final type of group is a dynamic group, which the name implies, the membership is generated by a formula each time the group is used.</p></li>
<li><p>A dynamic group includes any recipient in Active Directory with attribute values that match its filter.</p></li>
<li><p>If a recipient's properties are modified to match the filter, the recipient could inadvertently become a group member and start receiving messages that are sent to the group.</p></li>
<li><p>Well-defined, consistent account provisioning processes will reduce the chances of this issue occurring.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image49.webp" style="width:7.03656in;height:3.70961in" alt="Screenshot of the Dynamic Group membership rule generator. Set up a rule that includes members from a specific place." /></p>
<p>This dynamic group would consist of all valid members of the Microsoft Entra ID.</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Configure and manage device registration

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><p>With the proliferation of devices of all shapes and sizes and the proliferation of bring-your-own-device (BYOD), IT professionals are faced with two somewhat opposing goals:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Allow end users to be productive wherever and whenever and on any device</p></li>
<li><p>Protect the organization's assets</p></li>
</ul>
<p>To protect these assets, IT-staff needs to first manage the device identities. IT-staff can build on the device identity with tools like Microsoft Intune to ensure standards for security and compliance are met. Microsoft Entra ID enables single sign-on to devices, apps, and services from anywhere through these devices.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Your users get access to your organization's assets they need.</p></li>
<li><p>Your IT-staff gets the controls they need to secure your organization.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Microsoft Entra registered devices</strong></p>
<p>The goal of Microsoft Entra registered devices is to provide your users with support for the BYOD or mobile device scenarios. In these scenarios, a user can access your organization’s Microsoft Entra ID controlled resources using a personal device.</p>
<p>Expand table</p>
<table style="width:92%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 20%" />
<col style="width: 71%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><strong>Microsoft Entra registered</strong></th>
<th style="text-align: left;"><strong>Description</strong></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Definition</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Registered to Microsoft Entra ID without requiring organizational account to sign in to the device</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Primary audience</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Applicable to Bring your own device (BYOD), and Mobile devices</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device ownership</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">User or Organization</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Operating systems</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Windows 10, Windows 11, iOS, Android, and macOS</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device sign in options</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">End-user local credentials, Password, Windows Hello, PIN Biometrics</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device management</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Mobile Device Management (example: Microsoft Intune)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Key capabilities</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">SSO to cloud resources, Conditional Access</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image51.webp" style="width:1.77009in;height:2.4434in" alt="Diagram of Microsoft Entra registered devices. Shows a laptop and cell registered." /></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra registered devices are signed in to using a local account like a Microsoft account on a Windows 10 device, but additionally have a Microsoft Entra account attached for access to organizational resources. Access to resources in the organization can be further limited based on that Microsoft Entra account and Conditional Access policies applied to the device identity.</p></li>
<li><p>Administrators can secure and further control these Microsoft Entra registered devices using Mobile Device Management (MDM) tools like Microsoft Intune. MDM provides a means to enforce organization-required configurations like requiring storage to be encrypted, password complexity, and security software kept updated.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID registration can be accomplished when accessing a work application for the first time or manually using the Windows 10 Settings menu.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Scenarios for registered devices</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>A user in your organization wants to access tools for email, reporting time-off, and benefits enrollment from their home PC.</p></li>
<li><p>Your organization has these tools behind a Conditional Access policy that requires access from an Intune compliant device.</p></li>
<li><p>The user adds their organization account and registers their home PC with Microsoft Entra ID and the required Intune policies are enforced giving the user access to their resources.</p></li>
<li><p>Another user wants to access their organizational email on their personal Android phone that has been rooted.</p></li>
<li><p>Your company requires a compliant device and has created an Intune compliance policy to block any rooted devices. The employee is stopped from accessing organizational resources on this device.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong><mark>Microsoft Entra joined devices</mark></strong></p>
<p>Microsoft Entra join is intended for organizations that want to be cloud-first or cloud-only. Any organization can deploy Microsoft Entra joined devices no matter the size or industry. Microsoft Entra join enables access to both cloud and on-premises apps and resources.</p>
<p>Expand table</p>
<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 16%" />
<col style="width: 78%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><strong>Microsoft Entra joined</strong></th>
<th style="text-align: left;"><strong>Description</strong></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Definition</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Joined only to Microsoft Entra ID requiring organizational account to sign in to the device</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Primary audience</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Suitable for both cloud-only and hybrid organizations</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device ownership</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Organization</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Operating systems</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">All Windows 10 &amp; 11 devices except Windows 10/11 Home</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device management</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Mobile Device Management (example: Microsoft Intune)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Key capabilities</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">SSO to both cloud and on-premises resources, Conditional Access, Self-service Password Reset and Windows Hello PIN reset</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<ul>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra joined devices are signed into using an organizational Microsoft Entra account.</p></li>
<li><p>Access to resources in the organization can be further limited based on that Microsoft Entra account and Conditional Access policies applied to the device identity.</p></li>
<li><p>Administrators can secure and further control Microsoft Entra joined devices using Mobile Device Management (MDM) tools like Microsoft Intune or in co-management scenarios using Microsoft Endpoint Configuration Manager.</p></li>
<li><p>These tools provide a means to enforce organization-required configurations like requiring storage to be encrypted, password complexity, software installations, and software updates. Administrators can make organization applications available to Microsoft Entra joined devices using Configuration Manager.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra join can be accomplished using self-service options like the Out of Box Experience (OOBE), bulk enrollment, or Windows Autopilot.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra joined devices can still maintain single sign-on access to on-premises resources when they are on the organization's network. Microsoft Entra joined devices authenticate to on-premises servers like for file, print, and other applications.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Scenarios for joined devices</strong></p>
<p>Although Microsoft Entra join is primarily intended for organizations that do not have an on-premises Windows Server Active Directory infrastructure, you can certainly use it in scenarios where:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>You want to transition to cloud-based infrastructure using Microsoft Entra ID and MDM like Intune.</p></li>
<li><p>You can’t use an on-premises domain join, for example, if you need to get mobile devices such as tablets and phones under control.</p></li>
<li><p>Your users primarily need to access Microsoft 365 or other SaaS apps integrated with Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
<li><p>You want to manage a group of users in Microsoft Entra ID instead of in Active Directory. This scenario can apply, for example, to seasonal workers, contractors, or students.</p></li>
<li><p>You want to provide joining capabilities to workers in remote branch offices with limited on-premises infrastructure.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>You can configure Microsoft Entra joined devices for all Windows 10 devices with the exception of Windows 10 Home.</p>
<p>The goal of Microsoft Entra joined devices is to simplify:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Windows deployments of work-owned devices</p></li>
<li><p>Access to organizational apps and resources from any Windows device</p></li>
<li><p>Cloud-based management of work-owned devices</p></li>
<li><p>Users to sign in to their devices with their Microsoft Entra ID or synced Active Directory work or school accounts.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image52.webp" style="width:3.66845in;height:3in" alt="Diagram of Microsoft Entra joined devices connected to the cloud. A laptop registered to your cloud directory." /></p>
<p>Microsoft Entra Join can be deployed by using a number of different methods.</p>
<p><strong><mark>Hybrid Microsoft Entra joined devices</mark></strong></p>
<p>For more than a decade, many organizations have used the domain join to their on-premises Active Directory to enable:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>IT departments to manage work-owned devices from a central location.</p></li>
<li><p>Users to sign in to their devices with their Active Directory work or school accounts.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Typically, organizations with an on-premises footprint rely on imaging methods to configure devices, and they often use <strong>Configuration Manager</strong> or <strong>group policy (GP)</strong> to manage them.</p>
<p>If your environment has an on-premises AD footprint and you also want benefit from the capabilities provided by Microsoft Entra ID, you can implement hybrid Microsoft Entra joined devices.</p>
<p>These devices are devices that are joined to your on-premises Active Directory and registered with your Microsoft Entra directory.</p>
<p>Expand table</p>
<table style="width:96%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 20%" />
<col style="width: 75%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><strong>Hybrid Microsoft Entra joined</strong></th>
<th style="text-align: left;"><strong>Description</strong></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Definition</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Joined to on-premises AD and Microsoft Entra ID requiring organizational account to sign in to the device</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Primary audience</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Suitable for hybrid organizations with existing on-premises AD infrastructure</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device ownership</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Organization</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Operating systems</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Windows 11, 10, 8.1 and 7, along with Windows Server 2008/R2, 2012/R2, 2016 and 2019</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device sign in options</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Password or Windows Hello for Business</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Device management</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Group Policy, Configuration Manager standalone or co-management with Microsoft Intune</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;">Key capabilities</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">SSO to both cloud and on-premises resources, Conditional Access, Self-service Password Reset and Windows Hello PIN reset</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image53.webp" style="width:3.05893in;height:2.59434in" alt="Diagram of the process flow of Hybrid Microsoft Entra joined devices. A laptop is registered to an on-premises active directory." /></p>
<p><strong>Scenarios for hybrid joined</strong></p>
<p>Use Microsoft Entra hybrid joined devices if:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>You have Win32 apps deployed to these devices that rely on Active Directory machine authentication.</p></li>
<li><p>You want to continue to use Group Policy to manage device configuration.</p></li>
<li><p>You want to continue to use existing imaging solutions to deploy and configure devices.</p></li>
<li><p>You must support down-level Windows 7 and 8.1 devices in addition to Windows 10.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Device Writeback</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>In a cloud-based Microsoft Entra ID configuration, devices are only registered in Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
<li><p>Your on-premises AD has no visibility of the devices. This means that conditional access in the cloud is easy to set up and maintain.</p></li>
<li><p>However, in this section we are discussing hybrid setups with Microsoft Entra Connect.</p></li>
<li><p>How can you do on-premises conditional access using devices, if they only exist in Microsoft Entra ID?</p></li>
<li><p>Device writeback helps you to keep a track of devices registered with Microsoft Entra ID in AD.</p></li>
<li><p>You will have a copy of the device objects in the container "Registered Devices"</p></li>
<li><p>Scenario: You have an application that you want to give access to users only if they are coming from registered devices.</p></li>
<li><p>Cloud: You can write Conditional Access policies for any Microsoft Entra integrated applications to authorize based on whether the device is joined to Microsoft Entra ID or not.</p></li>
<li><p>On-premises: This is not possible without device writeback. If the application is integrated with ADFS (2012 or higher) then you can write claim rules to check for the device status and then provide access only if the "is managed " claim is present.</p></li>
<li><p>In order to issue this claim, ADFS will check for the device object in the "Registered Devices" container and then issue the claim accordingly.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Windows Hello For Business (WHFB) requires device writeback to function in Hybrid and Federated scenarios.</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Manage Licenses

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><ul>
<li><p>Microsoft paid cloud services, such as Microsoft 365, Enterprise Mobility + Security, Dynamics 365, and other similar products, require licenses.</p></li>
<li><p>These licenses are assigned to each user who needs access to these services.</p></li>
<li><p>To manage licenses, administrators use one of the management portals (Office or Azure) and PowerShell cmdlets. Microsoft Entra ID is the underlying infrastructure that supports identity management for all Microsoft cloud services.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID stores information about license assignment states for users.</p></li>
<li><p>Until now, licenses could only be assigned at the individual user level, which can make large-scale management difficult.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, to add or remove user licenses based on organizational changes, such as users joining or leaving the organization or a department, an administrator often must write a complex PowerShell script.</p></li>
<li><p>This script makes individual calls to the cloud service.</p></li>
<li><p>To address those challenges, Microsoft Entra ID now includes group-based licensing.</p></li>
<li><p>You can assign one or more product licenses to a group.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID ensures that the licenses are assigned to all members of the group.</p></li>
<li><p>Any new members who join the group are assigned the appropriate licenses. When they leave the group, those licenses are removed.</p></li>
<li><p>This licensing management eliminates the need for automating license management via PowerShell to reflect changes in the organization and departmental structure on a per-user basis.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>License requirements</strong></p>
<p>You must have one of the following licenses to use group-based licensing:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Paid or trial subscription for Microsoft Entra ID Premium P1 and above</p></li>
<li><p>Paid or trial edition of Office 365 Enterprise E3 or Office 365 A3 or Office 365 GCC G3 or Office 365 E3 for GCCH or Office 365 E3 for DOD and above</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Required number of licenses</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>For any groups assigned a license, you must also have a license for each unique member.</p></li>
<li><p>While you don't have to assign each member of the group a license, you must have at least enough licenses to include all of the members.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, if you have 1,000 unique members who are part of licensed groups in your tenant, you must have at least 1,000 licenses to meet the licensing agreement.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Features</strong></p>
<p>Here are the main features of group-based licensing:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Licenses can be assigned to any security group in Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
<li><p>Security groups can be synced from on-premises, by using Microsoft Entra Connect.</p></li>
<li><p>You can also create security groups directly in Microsoft Entra ID (also called cloud-only groups), or automatically via the Microsoft Entra dynamic group feature.</p></li>
<li><p>When a product license is assigned to a group, the administrator can disable one or more service plans in the product. Typically, this assignment is done when the organization is not yet ready to start using a service included in a product.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, the administrator might assign Microsoft 365 to a department, but temporarily disable the Yammer service.</p></li>
<li><p>All Microsoft cloud services that require user-level licensing are supported. This support includes all Microsoft 365 products, Enterprise Mobility + Security, and Dynamics 365.</p></li>
<li><p>Group-based licensing is currently available only through the <a href="https://portal.azure.com/">Azure portal</a>. (coming soon to the Microsoft Entra admin center)</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID automatically manages license modifications that result from group membership changes. Typically, license modifications are effective within minutes of a membership change.</p></li>
<li><p>A user can be a member of multiple groups with license policies specified. A user can also have some licenses that were directly assigned, outside of any groups. The resulting user state is a combination of all assigned product and service licenses. If a user is assigned same license from multiple sources, the license will be consumed only once.</p></li>
<li><p>In some cases, licenses cannot be assigned to a user. For example, there might not be enough available licenses in the tenant, or conflicting services might have been assigned at the same time. Administrators have access to information about users for whom Microsoft Entra ID could not fully process group licenses. They can then take corrective action based on that information.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Some Microsoft services are not available in all locations. The administrator, before assigning a license to a user, should specify usage location in the User Profile.</p>
<p>For group license assignment, any users without a usage location specified inherit the location of the directory. If you have users in multiple locations, we recommend that you always set usage location as part of your user creation flow in Microsoft Entra ID (for example, via Microsoft Entra Connect configuration) - that ensures the result of license assignment is always correct and users do not receive services in locations that are not allowed.</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### Exercise – Change group license assignments

<table style="width:99%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 99%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th style="text-align: left;"><p><strong>Change group license assignment</strong></p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>Open <a href="https://entra.microsoft.com/">https://entra.microsoft.com</a> to get to the Microsoft Entra admin center.</p></li>
<li><p>In the left navigation, open <strong>Groups</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Select <strong>All groups</strong>, then select one of the available groups.</p></li>
<li><p>In the left navigation, under <strong>Manage</strong>, select <strong>Licenses</strong>.</p></li>
</ol>
<p>You will see a list of any license assignments that are currently made. And you will find that you have to use the Microsoft 365 Admin Center to make any updates.</p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>Review the current assignments and then, on the menu, select <strong>+ Assignments</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>Open <a href="https://admin.microsoft.com/">https://admin.microsoft.com</a> to open the Microsoft 365 admin center.</p></li>
<li><p>Select <strong>Billing</strong>. Then select <strong>Licenses</strong>."</p></li>
<li><p>Select an available license from the list.</p></li>
<li><p>Select <strong>Groups</strong> from the menu near the top of the page.</p></li>
<li><p>Select the <strong>+ Assign licenses</strong> option.</p></li>
<li><p>Pick the group you were looking at earlier in Microsoft Entra. Then select the <strong>Assign</strong> button at the bottom of the page.</p></li>
<li><p>On the group’s Licenses page, review the change. You should be able to see the change in both the Microsoft Entra admin center and the Microsoft 365 admin center.</p></li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Identify and resolve license assignment problems for a group in Microsoft Entra ID</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Group-based licensing in Microsoft Entra ID introduces the concept of users in a licensing error state. In this section, we explain the reasons why users might end up in this state.</p></li>
<li><p>When you assign licenses directly to individual users, without using group-based licensing, the assignment operation might fail.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, when you execute the PowerShell cmdlet Set-MgUserLicense on a user object, the cmdlet can fail for many reasons that are related to business logic.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, there might be an insufficient number of licenses or a conflict between two service plans that can't be assigned at the same time. The problem is immediately reported back to you.</p></li>
<li><p>When you're using group-based licensing the same errors can occur, but they happen in the background while the Microsoft Entra service is assigning licenses.</p></li>
<li><p>For this reason, the errors can't be communicated to you immediately. Instead, they're recorded on the user object and then reported via the administrative portal.</p></li>
<li><p>The original intent to license the user is never lost, but it's recorded in an error state for future investigation and resolution.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Not enough licenses</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Problem</strong>: There aren't enough available licenses for one of the products that's specified in the group. You need to either purchase more licenses for the product or free up unused licenses from other users or groups.</p></li>
<li><p>To see how many licenses are available, go to <strong>Microsoft Entra - Identity - Billing</strong> , then <strong>Licenses</strong>, then <strong>All products</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>To see which users and groups are consuming licenses, select a product. Under <strong>Licensed users</strong>, you see a list of all users who have had licenses assigned directly or via one or more groups. Under <strong>Licensed groups</strong>, you see all groups with product licenses assigned.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>PowerShell</strong>: PowerShell cmdlets report this error as <em>CountViolation</em>.</p>
<p><strong>Service plans that conflict</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Problem</strong>: One of the products that's specified in the group contains a service plan that conflicts with another service plan that's already assigned to the user via a different product.</p></li>
<li><p>Some service plans are configured in a way that they can't be assigned to the same user as another, related service plan.</p></li>
<li><p>Consider the following example. A user has a license for Office 365 Enterprise <em>E1</em> assigned directly, with all the plans enabled. The user has been added to a group that has the Office 365 Enterprise <em>E3</em> product assigned to it. The E3 product contains service plans that can't overlap with the plans that are included in E1, so the group license assignment fails with the <strong>Conflicting service plans</strong> error. In this example, the conflicting service plans are:</p></li>
<li><p>SharePoint Online (Plan 2) conflicts with SharePoint Online (Plan 1).</p></li>
<li><p>Exchange Online (Plan 2) conflicts with Exchange Online (Plan 1).</p></li>
<li><p>To solve this conflict, you need to disable two of the plans. You can disable the E1 license that's directly assigned to the user.</p></li>
<li><p>Or, you need to modify the entire group license assignment and disable the plans in the E3 license. Alternatively, you might decide to remove the E1 license from the user if it's redundant in the context of the E3 license.</p></li>
<li><p>The decision about how to resolve conflicting product licenses always belongs to the administrator. Microsoft Entra ID doesn't automatically resolve license conflicts.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>PowerShell</strong>: PowerShell cmdlets report this error as <em>MutuallyExclusiveViolation</em>.</p>
<p><strong>Other products depend on this license</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Problem</strong>: One of the products that's specified in the group contains a service plan that must be enabled for another service plan, in another product, to function. This error occurs when Microsoft Entra ID attempts to remove the underlying service plan. For example, this can happen when you remove the user from the group.</p></li>
<li><p>To solve this problem, you need to make sure that the required plan is still assigned to users through some other method or that the dependent services are disabled for those users. After doing that, you can properly remove the group license from those users.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>PowerShell</strong>: PowerShell cmdlets report this error as <em>DependencyViolation</em>.</p>
<p><strong>Usage location isn't allowed</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Problem</strong>: Some Microsoft services aren't available in all locations because of local laws and regulations. Before you can assign a license to a user, you must specify the <strong>Usage location</strong> property for the user. You can specify the location under the <strong>User</strong>, then <strong>Profile</strong>, then <strong>Edit</strong> the section in the Azure portal.</p></li>
<li><p>When Microsoft Entra ID attempts to assign a group license to a user whose usage location isn't supported, it fails and records an error on the user.</p></li>
<li><p>To solve this problem, remove users from unsupported locations from the licensed group. Alternatively, if the current usage location values don't represent the actual user location, you can modify them so that the licenses are correctly assigned next time (if the new location is supported).</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>PowerShell</strong>: PowerShell cmdlets report this error as <em>ProhibitedInUsageLocationViolation</em>.</p>
<p><strong> Note</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>When Microsoft Entra ID assigns group licenses, any users without a specified usage location inherit the location of the directory. We recommend that administrators set the correct usage location values on users before using group-based licensing to comply with local laws and regulations.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Duplicate proxy addresses</strong></p>
<p>If you use Exchange Online, some users in your organization might be incorrectly configured with the same proxy address value. When group-based licensing tries to assign a license to such a user, it fails and shows “Proxy address is already being used.”</p>
<p>After you resolve any proxy address problems for the affected users, make sure to force license processing on the group to ensure that the licenses can now be applied.</p>
<p><strong>Microsoft Entra Mail and ProxyAddresses attribute change</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Problem</strong>: While updating license assignment on a user or a group, you might see that the Microsoft Entra Mail and ProxyAddresses attribute of some users are changed.</p></li>
<li><p>Updating license assignment on a user causes the proxy address calculation to be triggered, which can change user attributes.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>LicenseAssignmentAttributeConcurrencyException in audit logs</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p><strong>Problem</strong>: User has LicenseAssignmentAttributeConcurrencyException for license assignment in audit logs. When group-based licensing tries to process concurrent license assignment of the same license to a user, this exception is recorded on the user. This typically happens when a user is a member of more than one group with same assigned license. Microsoft Entra ID will retry processing the user license and will resolve the issue. There is no action required from the customer to fix this issue.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>More than one product license assigned to a group</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>You can assign more than one product license to a group. For example, you can assign Office 365 Enterprise E3 and Enterprise Mobility + Security to a group to easily enable all included services for users.</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Entra ID attempts to assign all licenses that are specified in the group to each user. If Microsoft Entra ID can't assign one of the products because of business logic problems, it won't assign the other licenses in the group either. An example is if there aren't enough licenses for all, or if there are conflicts with other services that are enabled on the user.</p></li>
<li><p>You can see the users who failed to get assigned and check which products are affected by this problem.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>When a licensed group is deleted</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>You must remove all licenses assigned to a group before you can delete the group. However, removing licenses from all the users in the group may take time. There can be failures if user has a dependent license assigned. If a user has a license that is dependent on a license which is being removed due to group deletion, the license assignment to the user is converted from inherited to direct.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, consider a group that has Office 365 E3/E5 assigned with a Skype for Business service plan enabled. Also imagine that a few members of the group have Audio Conferencing licenses assigned directly. When the group is deleted, group-based licensing will try to remove Office 365 E3/E5 from all users. Because Audio Conferencing is dependent on Skype for Business, for any users with Audio Conferencing assigned, group-based licensing converts the Office 365 E3/E5 licenses to direct license assignment.</p></li>
</ul>
<ol start="9" type="1">
<li><p><strong>Manage licenses for products with prerequisites</strong></p></li>
</ol>
<ul>
<li><p>Some Microsoft Online products you might own are <em>add-ons</em>. Add-ons require a prerequisite service plan to be enabled for a user or a group before they can be assigned a license. With group-based licensing, the system requires that both the prerequisite and add-on service plans be present in the same group to ensure that any users who are added to the group can receive the fully working product. Let's consider the following example:</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Workplace Analytics is an add-on product. It contains a single service plan with the same name. We can only assign this service plan to a user, or group, when one of the following prerequisites is also assigned:</p></li>
<li><p>Exchange Online (Plan 1)</p></li>
<li><p>Exchange Online (Plan 2)</p></li>
<li><p>If we try to assign this product on its own to a group, the portal returns a notification message. If we select the item details, it shows the following error message:</p></li>
<li><p>License operation failed. Make sure that the group has necessary services before adding or removing a dependent service. <strong>The service Microsoft Workplace Analytics requires Exchange Online (Plan 2) to be enabled as well</strong>.</p></li>
<li><p>To assign this add-on license to a group, we must ensure that the group also contains the prerequisite service plan. For example, we might update an existing group that already contains the full Office 365 E3 product, and then add the add-on product to it.</p></li>
<li><p>It is also possible to create a standalone group that contains only the minimum required products to make the add-on work. It can then be used to license only selected users for the add-on product. Based on the previous example, you would assign the following products to the same group:</p></li>
<li><p>Office 365 Enterprise E3 with only the Exchange Online (Plan 2) service plan enabled</p></li>
<li><p>Microsoft Workplace Analytics</p></li>
<li><p>From now on, any users added to this group consume one license of the E3 product and one license of the Workplace Analytics product. At the same time, those users can be members of another group that gives them the full E3 product, and they still consume only one license for that product.</p></li>
<li><p><strong>Tip</strong></p></li>
<li><p>You can create multiple groups for each prerequisite service plan. For example, if you use both Office 365 Enterprise E1 and Office 365 Enterprise E3 for your users, you can create two groups to license Microsoft Workplace Analytics: one that uses E1 as a prerequisite and the other that uses E3. This lets you distribute the add-on to E1 and E3 users without consuming additional licenses.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Force the group license process to resolve errors</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Depending on what steps you've taken to resolve the errors, it might be necessary to manually trigger the processing of a group to update the user state.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, if you free up some licenses by removing direct license assignments from users, you need to trigger the processing of groups that previously failed to fully license all user members. To reprocess a group, go to the group pane, open <strong>Licenses</strong>, and then select the <strong>Reprocess</strong> button on the toolbar.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Force the user license process to resolve errors</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>Depending on what steps you've taken to resolve the errors, it might be necessary to manually trigger the processing of a user to update the user's state.</p></li>
<li><p>For example, after you resolve duplicate proxy address problem for an affected user, you need to trigger the processing of the user. To reprocess a user, go to the user pane, open <strong>Licenses</strong>, and then select the <strong>Reprocess</strong> button on the toolbar.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>How to migrate users with individual licenses to group licenses</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><p>You may have existing licenses deployed to users in the organizations via direct assignment; that is, using PowerShell scripts or other tools to assign individual user licenses. Before you begin using group-based licensing to manage licenses in your organization, you can use this migration plan to seamlessly replace existing solutions with group-based licensing.</p></li>
<li><p>Keep in mind that you should avoid a situation in which migrating to group-based licensing will result in users temporarily losing their currently assigned licenses. Any process that may result in removal of licenses should be avoided to remove the risk of users losing access to services and their data.</p></li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Recommended migration process</strong></p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>You have existing automation (for example, PowerShell) managing license assignment and removal for users. Leave it running as is.</p></li>
<li><p>Create a new licensing group (or decide which existing groups to use) and make sure that all required users are added as members.</p></li>
<li><p>Assign the required licenses to those groups; your goal should be to reflect the same licensing state your existing automation (for example, PowerShell) is applying to those users.</p></li>
<li><p>Verify that licenses have been applied to all users in those groups. This application can be done by checking the processing state on each group and by checking Audit Logs.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>You can perform a random check of a few individual users by looking at their license details. You will see that they have the same licenses assigned “directly” and “inherited” from groups.</p></li>
<li><p>You can run a PowerShell script to <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/active-directory/enterprise-users/licensing-group-advanced">verify how licenses are assigned to users</a>.</p></li>
<li><p>When the same product license is assigned to the user both directly and through a group, only one license is consumed by the user. Hence no additional licenses are required to perform migration.</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p>Verify that no license assignments failed by checking each group for users in error state.</p></li>
</ol>
<p>Consider removing the original direct assignments. We recommend that you do it gradually, and monitor the outcome on a subset of users first. If you leave the original direct assignments on users, when the users leave their licensed groups they retain the directly assigned licenses, which might not be what you want.</p>
<p><strong>An example</strong></p>
<p>An organization has 1,000 users. All users require Office 365 Enterprise E3 licenses. Currently the organization has a PowerShell script running on premises, adding and removing licenses from users as they come and go. However, the organization wants to replace the script with group-based licensing so licenses can be managed automatically by Microsoft Entra ID.</p>
<p>Here is what the migration process could look like:</p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>Using the Azure portal, assign the Office 365 E3 license to the <strong>All users</strong> group in Microsoft Entra ID.</p></li>
<li><p>Confirm that license assignment has completed for all users. Go to the overview page for the group, select <strong>Licenses</strong>, and check the processing status at the top of the <strong>Licenses</strong> page.</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Look for “Latest license changes have been applied to all users" to confirm processing has completed.</p></li>
<li><p>Look for a notification on top about any users for whom licenses may have not been successfully assigned. Did we run out of licenses for some users? Do some users have conflicting license plans that prevent them from inheriting group licenses?</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p>You need to check a few users to verify that they have both the direct and group licenses applied. Go to the profile page for a user, select Licenses, and examine the state of licenses.</p></li>
</ol>
<ul>
<li><p>This is the expected user state during migration:</p></li>
</ul>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image54.webp" style="width:7.6875in;height:2.96736in" alt="Screenshot of the Licenses page. See the license has direct assignments to some users, and that it has inherited users from a group." /></p>
<ol type="1">
<li><p>After confirming that both direct and group licenses are equivalent, you can start removing direct licenses from users. You can test this by removing them for individual users in the portal and then run automation scripts to have them removed in bulk. Here is an example of the same user with the direct licenses removed through the portal. Notice that the license state remains unchanged, but we no longer see direct assignments.</p></li>
</ol>
<p><img src="subjects/tech/azure/az-104-elearn-portal-labs/img/media/image55.webp" style="width:7.6875in;height:2.94167in" alt="Screenshot of the Licenses page in Microsoft Entra ID after the migration is completed." /></p>
<p><strong>Change license assignments for a user or group in Microsoft Entra ID</strong></p>
<p>This section describes how to move users and groups between service license plans in Microsoft Entra ID. The goal is to ensure that there's no loss of service or data during the license change. Users should switch between services seamlessly. The license plan assignment steps in this section describe changing a user or group on Office 365 E1 to Office 365 E3, but the steps apply to all license plans. When you update license assignments for a user or group, the license assignment removals and new assignments are made simultaneously so that users do not lose access to their services during license changes or see license conflicts between plans.</p>
<p>Before you update the license assignments, verify certain assumptions are true for all of the users or groups to be updated. If the assumptions aren't true for all of the users in a group, the migration might fail for some. As a result, some of the users might lose access to services or data. Ensure that:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>Users have the current license plan that's assigned to a group and inherited by the user and not assigned directly.</p></li>
<li><p>You have enough available licenses for the license plan you're assigning. If you don't have enough licenses, some users might not be assigned the new license plan. You can check the number of available licenses.</p></li>
<li><p>Always confirm users don't have assigned service licenses that can conflict with the desired license or prevent removal of the current license. For example, a license from a service such as Workplace Analytics or Project Online that has a dependency on other services.</p></li>
<li><p>If you manage groups on-premises and sync them into Microsoft Entra ID via Microsoft Entra Connect, then you add or remove users by using your on-premises system. It can take some time for the changes to sync with Microsoft Entra ID to be picked up by group licensing.</p></li>
<li><p>If you're using Microsoft Entra dynamic group memberships, you add or remove users by changing their attributes, but the update process for license assignments remains the same.</p></li>
</ul></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
